# Preface.

<image-desc>A small centered icon of a crown above the start of the text.</image-desc>

How indispensable the prayer of faith is for obtaining all kinds of spiritual and physical blessings from above has been sufficiently shown by the Apostle *James* in Chapter 1, verses 5 through 8 <note>James 1:5–8 is a classic New Testament passage regarding the need for unwavering faith when asking God for wisdom.</note>, where he particularly demonstrates the impossibility of receiving anything from GOD due to the nature and characteristics of unbelief. For, he says, *He who doubts is like a wave of the sea, driven and tossed by the wind. Let not such a man think that he shall receive anything from the LORD. A doubter is unstable in all his ways.* Just as it is in nature—where the kind influences of heaven can be greatly hindered by violent, roaring sea-waves and stormy winds, preventing its <term>astrological light- and fire-power<note>In the 17th and 18th centuries, it was believed that the stars and planets transmitted a physical and spiritual energy—often called "influence"—to the earth through the air.</note></term> from infusing itself—so too the creatures that live in the sea (such as the pearl mussels) can never better open themselves to receive the dripping <term>heavenly dew<note>original: "Himmels-Thau." There was a long-standing belief in natural history that pearls were formed when mussels rose to the surface to catch the morning dew.</note></term> than when the sea is still and the atmosphere is serene and

<summary>This preface introduces the necessity of steadfast faith in prayer, using the biblical warnings of the Apostle James. The author employs a vivid metaphor from early modern natural philosophy, comparing a doubting soul to a stormy sea that cannot receive the nourishing light and dew of heaven.</summary>

<keywords>faith, prayer, James 1:5-8, unbelief, natural philosophy, astrological influence, heavenly dew, pearl mussels, spiritual stability</keywords>

# Preface.

is cleared: it is the same case with the restless and worldly-scattered desires of the heart of a person still standing in unbelief, who can obtain nothing from GOD as long as he does not yet quiet his heart in GOD through faith. The truth of this assertion <note>original: "Asserti"</note> will shine even more clearly before everyone’s eyes if we now, conversely, consider somewhat more specifically the properties of a faithful prayer, as well as the nature of our spirit created by GOD, and how far it can be moved by the grace of God to determine itself toward something in faith according to its free power of will. For one thing is certain: that GOD, in the Creation, imparted to our human spirit such a freedom of the will that it cannot be forced by any power to accept anything good or evil, except solely through the power and force <note>original: "Forçe"</note> of love, which alone bends the free will to stretch itself out in strong desire of faith toward those goods which God wishes to give, but which He can by no means give without such a consent of faith. Multiple examples in Holy Scripture prove this sufficiently. Mary was destined by GOD to essentially receive the highest treasure of the world—the divine from the Father, the Son of GOD—in her womb. Nevertheless, this could not happen before the preceding faithful assent of her free power of will. Therefore, the Angel Gabriel had to first, so to speak, go and fetch her consent. And as soon as Mary said: *Let it be unto me according to your word;* as soon as that happened, the door of faith was opened, so that the Holy Spirit gained access to introduce the divine workings of the wonderful conception of Christ within her. Whereas, by contrast, in the time of Christ, many thousands of people lost the goods brought by Him from heaven, and remained <note>This "remained" translates the catchword "blieben," which anticipates the first word of the following page.</note>

<meta>catchword: remained,</meta>

<summary>This page argues that God respects human free will and requires the "consent of faith" to bestow blessings, using the Annunciation to Mary as the definitive example of human cooperation with the divine.</summary>
<keywords>Free Will, Grace, Faith, Virgin Mary, Annunciation, Holy Spirit, Divine Love, Consent</keywords>

# Preface.

remained lost, as long as the faithful reaching out of the will toward such offered goods was lacking. Just as even Christ could not perform the slightest miracle among his countrymen in Nazareth because of their unbelief.

By virtue of this free <term>power of the will<note>original: "VVillens-Krafft"</note></term> of ours, God even today cannot deal with us in any other way; He cannot draw the strayed free spirit of man back into His <term>fellowship<note>original: "Gemeinschafft"</note></term> until that spirit, in the <term>desire of its will<note>original: "VVillens-Begierde"</note></term>, hungers and thirsts for lost <term>righteousness<note>original: "Gerechtigkeit"</note></term> through prayer and the longing of faith. It must direct its <term>imagination<note>In theosophical writing, "imagination" is not just fantasy, but a spiritual faculty used to focus the mind and "image" divine reality within oneself.</note></term> toward that which God Himself is active in His own desire of love: namely, to destroy the established kingdom of sin and, in its place, build up the kingdom of light and love.

It is, therefore, the earnest <term>desire of faith<note>original: "Glaubens-Begierde"</note></term> in those people—which expresses itself through a thousand sighs, cooings <note>An allusion to the "moaning of the dove," a common metaphor for the soul's yearning for God.</note>, and longings of the spirit—that serves as that mighty power. This power is communicated from the Word of God through the Holy Spirit and makes the heart capable of receiving God and His grace. For when the soul perceives its spiritual misery and evil and recognizes God's help, it draws the help of a heavenly Comforter to itself through its sighing. Conversely, the soul is removed further and further from such heavenly goods the less it desires to sigh for them.

And with this purpose in mind, these *Theosophical Heart-Devotions* have been composed with all diligence, so that one may increasingly strengthen oneself in the <term>knowledge<note>original: "Erkæntnis"</note></term> of those duties which our sole, true, and holy religion requires of us. For we recognize and confess only one single

# Preface.

**GOD** and Father, who according to his <term>Fire-property<note>original: "Feuers-Eigenschafft." In theosophical thought, this refers to God’s foundational, energetic power and justice, which must be tempered by "Light" to become manifest as love.</note></term> in the **Kingdom of Nature** is the eternal Root of **Creation**, and in Christ as the origin of the eternal **Light**, has willed the **Fire- and Light-Form** (from which all things consist) to appear also in **external nature**; he has therefore through his **Spirit** brought forth this **Fire- and Light-Principle** as an <term>astral salt-body<note>original: "astralischen Salz-Leib." In early modern spiritual alchemy, this refers to a semi-spiritual, crystalline substance that gives physical form to life.</note></term> or <term>Nitrum<note>Latin for saltpeter. Here it refers to a "divine salt" or active spiritual catalyst within material nature.</note></term>, and has made it visible in the **creatures**. We also know further, that we have a **LORD** and Savior, who wishes to refresh anew—with his **Light- and Love-Essence**—the poor human beings who, through the <term>Fall into Sin<note>original: "Sünden-Fall," referring to the biblical account of humanity's separation from God.</note></term>, lost the **divine light** and lay imprisoned in the harsh fire of an adverse sensation of the **wrath of GOD**; and through the **Holy Spirit** (as a **dew** falling down from heaven) he wishes to <term>impregnate<note>original: "beschvvængern." This biological metaphor describes the Holy Spirit fertilizing the soul so it can bear spiritual fruit.</note></term> and fill with light and life from **GOD** the **languishing earth of the heart** that lies under the curse.

Thus, everything in both the **Kingdom of Nature** and the **Kingdom of Grace** has its origin in this **divine fire, light**, and **regenerating spirit**, and everything finds its true rest once again in this, its origin. Necessarily, therefore, the only true service and the only true religion, with which we are obligated to the **original highest Essence** of all things, must consist in this: that we daily return into this, our origin. And since this is even in the **Kingdom of Nature** the noblest course of all **creatures**: so indeed it may also be the most perfect effort of men in the **Kingdom of Grace**, when they direct their course toward **GOD**, so that He is their highest wish and desire, delight and joy. In a word, our **divine service** consists in an <term>outstretched desire of faith<note>original: "Glaubens-Begierde." A central concept in this text, describing the soul’s active, hungry reaching out for God’s grace.</note></term> for **GOD** and in a **love-zealous worship of GOD** in the **Spirit**.

<meta>This page continues the preface's explanation of the spiritual utility of this prayer book, moving from the cosmological foundations of fire and light discussed on the previous page to the practical application of prayer as a means of returning the soul to God through self-denial.</meta>

# Preface.

Therefore, let everyone who takes these *Theosophical Heart-Devotions* into their hands consider them as an instruction that teaches us how to rightly practice the single duty of our true religion, and how to daily return into **GOD** as our origin through prayer. Ah! Indeed, a prayer of faith is nothing other than a constant presence of the mind, or as Paul says in *Hebrews 11:1*, a <term>substance<note>original Greek: "ὑπόστασις" (hypostasis). In this context, it refers to a firm confidence or the underlying spiritual reality that supports faith.</note></term> of the pious children of **God** in **GOD**, by virtue of which the soul—with its desire for faith and love—is not only constantly directed toward **GOD** as its origin, but other glorious exercises of religious duties are also linked with it. For a soul that constantly stretches out toward **GOD** in its prayer must necessarily strive to rid itself of the bonds of the world and the flesh, so that it may introduce its desires of faith all the more powerfully into **GOD**. Next, linked with such a desire of love for **GOD** in the prayer of the spirit, is also the well-ordered love for ourselves, which consists in the true denial of self. For by means of self-denial, we attain what we seek in the love of **God** for our own peace—namely, communion with **GOD**, our true happiness, and the peace of our souls. And when we enter into self-denial, as if into a spiritual <term>putrefaction<note>original Latin: "Putrefaction." In theosophical and alchemical thought, this refers to the necessary decay or "breaking down" of the old, selfish nature so that a new, spiritual life can be born.</note></term>, death, and the dying away of our own natural powers and desires of the will: we are then capable of receiving the balm of a quasi-heavenly and all-renewing salt from the working of the Holy Spirit. For this is actually the living seed of the spoken divine Word, from which the <term>new man<note>A biblical concept (Ephesians 4:24) referring to the regenerated spiritual nature of a person after their inner conversion.</note></term> grows forth in inner holiness, purity, and innocence of heart, and also powerfully proves himself externally through a quiet, peaceful, righteous, honorable, loving, and virtuous conduct.

<meta>catchword: Out</meta>

<summary>This page defines prayer as a constant state of the soul returning to God, its origin, through the process of self-denial. It uses the metaphor of alchemical "putrefaction" to describe the death of the old self, which allows the Holy Spirit to grow a "new man" characterized by inner purity and virtuous outward conduct.</summary>
<keywords>prayer, self-denial, spiritual rebirth, putrefaction, Holy Spirit, the new man, inner holiness, Hebrews 11:1, hypostasis</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the preface, explaining that the "Heart-Devotions" are intended to transcend sectarian divisions and external ceremonies. # Preface.

From all of this, however, it is now clear that in the use of these *Theosophical Heart-Devotions*, we have no intention of allowing ourselves to be hindered in the least by any artificial external form, liturgical constitution, ceremony, or religious opinion, through which the common mass of Christendom has unfortunately been divided and torn apart from one another for a long time in countless and wretched ways.

For because the people who are **Christ’s** own possession have been gathered from all tribes, heathens, and languages, and have this single necessary goal: that through **Christ** and in **Christ** they may return to their rest in **GOD**, from which we departed through the <term>Fall<note>original German: "Sünden-Fall." This refers to the biblical "Fall of Man," the transition of the first humans from a state of innocent obedience to God to a state of guilty disobedience.</note></term>. Thus, such a people, stretching out toward **GOD** in true <term>desire of faith<note>original German: "Glaubens-Begierde." A deep, active longing or spiritual hunger for God that characterizes the internal life of the believer.</note></term>, will not allow themselves to be taken captive by any external religious opinion or sectarian attachment. They know that in **Christ** only the <term>new creature<note>original German: "neue Creatur." A reference to Galatians 6:15, emphasizing that internal spiritual rebirth is more important than external rituals like circumcision.</note></term> matters, and otherwise neither circumcision nor uncircumcision, nor any other religious concepts. The single sign by which they recognize themselves and distinguish themselves from others—and likewise through which they enter into a <term>communion<note>original German: "Gemeinschafft." A fellowship or spiritual community of believers.</note></term> together—is their love for one another. Outwardly, however, this true Christian people does not desire to distinguish itself further from other people in ceremonies and divine service, except only in two external, innocent church ordinances which their **LORD** Himself commanded them. For according to His command, when they have become believers, they are accustomed to be immersed in water in the name of the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit; and at their external gatherings, as fellow members of **God’s** household, they all eat of one bread and all drink of one wine and from one cup. Which they then do in remembrance of their **Redeemer** and His [work] for them and for the salvation of their souls—

<summary>The author argues that true Christianity is defined by inner spiritual rebirth and mutual love rather than sectarian labels or complex ceremonies. It identifies baptism and communal bread and wine as the only two essential outward ordinances for believers.</summary>
<keywords>non-sectarianism, New Creature, Christian love, baptism, Lord's Supper, the Fall, spiritual rest, communion</keywords>

And this return of their **Lord**, for which they all wait with such great longing, they consider to be the time when their complete <term>redemption<note>original German: "Erloesung." In this context, it refers to the final deliverance from the physical world and sin at the end of time.</note></term> draws near, and they shall find perfect rest in **GOD** in a <term>glorified body<note>original German: "verklärten Leibe." The belief in a transformed, spiritual body that believers receive in the afterlife or upon Christ's return.</note></term>. Toward this goal, indeed, all our efforts of worship, our heart-devotions, sighs, and longings must be directed daily.

May the **LORD** therefore also bless these **Theosophical Heart-Devotions** for this purpose, and grant that the most noble workings of the Holy Spirit in my heart and the hearts of all people may at all times be a daily <term>cooing<note>original German: "Girren." A common Pietist metaphor used to describe the soul’s tender, mournful, and persistent prayer or longing for God, resembling the sound of a dove.</note></term>, sighing, and longing for the communion of **GOD** in **Christ JESUS** and for the true rest in our **Redeemer**

# Daily
## Rule of Life,
<note>original German: "Richt-Schnur." Literally a "straight-line" or "plumb line" used by builders, here used metaphorically as a spiritual guide or standard to live by.</note>

->*According to which a God-devoted soul seeks to collect itself with its senses and powers of mind every day in three special hours of devotion, and to turn into GOD for its rest.*<-

---

->Daniel 6:10<-

> *And Daniel knelt on his knees three times a day, prayed, praised, and gave thanks to his GOD.*

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'F' set within a square frame with leafy flourishes, topped by a small crown.</image-desc>

**F**ocus yourself, dear soul, and seek your rest, which you will find nowhere outside of **GOD**, neither in yourself nor in the whole world. Imagine your **GOD** as the center of silent and peaceful eternity, where not the slightest variations or changes take place. But consider also all creatures in the <term>circle of time<note>original German: "Zeit-Kreyfs." A concept in mystical philosophy representing the temporal, material world which revolves around the fixed, eternal center of God.</note></term> of this never-resting and fleeting world, and you will find how everything stands, as it were, in an ascending and descending motion around the divine point of rest of eternity. As long as time has not yet been swallowed up by eternity, everything will remain in a constant <term>circulation<note>original German: "Circulation." This refers to the spiritual and physical cycles of life, often associated in 17th-century thought with the idea that all things flow out from God and must eventually return to Him.</note></term> and, according to the words of the Holy Scripture, go *from **GOD**, through **GOD**, and to **GOD**.* Romans 11:36.

<meta>catchword: You</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the "Daily Rule of Life," a devotional guide encouraging the reader to pray three times a day following the biblical example of Daniel. It establishes a mystical worldview where God is the unmoving center of eternity while the world exists in a state of constant, restless motion.</summary>
<keywords>devotional life, Daniel, eternity, rest, circle of time, spiritual circulation, Romans 11:36</keywords>

You see, my heart, how everything, as it were, in which we live, move, and are sustained, descends from GOD, and communicates itself to all creatures for their increase, growth, and reproduction as a heavenly or <term>astral salt-body<note>original German: "astralischer Salz-Leib." In the mystical chemistry of the period (inspired by Paracelsus and Jacob Böhme), "Salt" represents the principle of materiality or "body" that allows spirit to take form. "Astral" refers to the celestial influences of the stars.</note></term>; it descends spiritually and becomes ever more physical the closer it comes to the center of the earth. Oh behold here, my soul, the beautiful **chain of creatures**, how it finally reaches us in its descent from GOD: *GOD hears the heavens, the heavens hear the earth, the earth hears the grain, fodder, and oil, and grain, fodder, and oil hear Jezreel. Hosea 2:21, 22.* <note>original: "GOTT erhært den Himmel, der Himmel erhært die Erde, die Erde erhært Korn, Mast und Oel, und Korn, Mast und Oel erhært Jesrael." The author is citing a passage where "hearing" (or answering) represents the interconnected flow of divine provision through the hierarchy of nature.</note>
But right here, at this descending chain, you find among the creatures that everything is in motion and nothing is at rest. Everything stands in a constant <term>birth of anguish<note>original German: "Angst-Geburt." A term from Jacob Böhme’s theosophy describing the restless, contracting struggle of nature as it strives to bring forth life or light from darkness.</note></term>, and the creatures go from one transformation into another. What nourishes the whole world comes from GOD, goes from one hand into another in a steady descending and ascending movement, and will not stand still until it completes its **circle**, stepping back from the physical into the spiritual, and finally coming from the last hand back to GOD. Now, dear soul, you too must learn, following the example of all creatures, to enter upon such a noble course in the spirit. You indeed have a heavenly, even a divine origin, and in the world you will find less and less rest the further you are removed from your origin, and the more you have gone out from the spiritual and heavenly into the physical and earthly. You have departed from the light of GOD in the lamentable Fall into sin. But if you direct your course toward Christ in your **desire of faith**, you will come back into the heavenly **Light-essence**, wherein the immortal spirit can set its foot and throne for eternal strengthening through faith, like a pillar in the temple of GOD. Then you will dwell in the hut of peace and in magnificent

Rest. If you swing yourself upward through faith from the abyss of earthly life, the realm of nature, and this world, into the light- and spirit-life of GOD: then you stand with your Creator and Redeemer upon one ground and in one **Center**, and the self-sufficient truth and freedom in GOD will, through the transcendence over nature and creature, make you free from the bondage of sins and from the law of death, which until now has prevented your peace. Indeed, your whole life must be such an upward-climbing of the <term>mind<note>original German: "Gemüth." A central term in German mysticism referring to the deep seat of emotions, will, and soul-consciousness.</note></term> to reach your rest in the original **Essence**. Yet it is also certain that every day has **three great hours**, which in their peculiar alternations can serve as a powerful guide for you to swing yourself in faith toward your point of rest in the Center of Eternity. Now, my spirit, lift yourself up from the airs of this transitory earth, and surrender yourself in faithful devotion to Him in whose communion you would wish to be eternally.

) ( ✾ ( )
B 3

...to glorify and to proclaim your honor. Your **wondrous goodness** has also become great toward me today, in that you graciously let me gaze upon your sunlight once more. O, how many during this night must have exchanged their natural sleep for the **sleep of death**, and how many therein perhaps have sunk into the terrible darkness of a hellish night of death—into a black, fearful, and despair-filled eternity, wherein the light never shines for them? Your merciful love toward me is therefore precious and to be highly esteemed. For as I now awake again from my sleep, it is as much to me as if you had awakened me from the dark **night of the grave**. Indeed, I must certainly imagine that every morning, when you grant me life anew, you have also created me anew, just as you led me forth from the deep night of a dark <term>nothingness<note>In mystical theology, "nothingness" (Nichts) refers to the state before creation, but also the state of humility required to receive God's light.</note></term>. 

My God, at this **new creation** which you perform with me today, I think of the **first creation** and of the blessed **state of Paradise** and innocence into which man arrived when he first awoke into this life. With the first sight of this great **world-realm**, his free spirit saw himself appointed as lord and king over all these earthly splendors. All the fruits of the outer world stood ready for his enjoyment. But there was nothing therein so pleasant that it should have captured this free spirit or drawn it away from the love of God. Rather, he delighted solely in the unspeakable **love of God** and ruled over the external **realm of nature** in a constant feeling of heavenly delight. In short, Adam was both servant and lord: a servant of God, and a lord over the entire world; he utilized the creatures for his service, and he stirred his heart for the service of God. O! what was that other than...

That we poor children of Adam lost this glory, and do not know how to pray and work in such pure innocence as Adam did. Ah, Lord Jesus, bring us back into the right order from which we departed through the lamentable Fall of Sin, for the sake of your love. Amen!

## Psalm 104.

->*A Praise of God from the Book of Nature.*<-

Bless the Lord, my soul. Lord my God, you are very glorious; you are beautifully and mightily adorned.

Light is your garment that you wear; you stretch out the heavens like a tapestry. <note>The German *Teppich* (tapestry/carpet) reflects the idea of the sky as a decorative fabric spread over the world.</note>

You arch it above with water; you ride upon the clouds as if on a chariot, and walk upon the wings of the wind.

You who make your angels into winds, and your servants into flames of fire.

You who ground the earth upon its foundations, so that it remains forever and ever.

You cover it with the deep as with a garment, and water stands above the mountains.

But at your rebuke they flee; at your thunder they hasten away.

The mountains rise up high, and the valleys sink down to the place which you have established for them.

You have set a boundary that they may not cross, and they must not cover the earth again.

You let springs gush forth in the valleys, so that the waters flow between the mountains.

So that all the beasts of the field may drink, and the wild creatures may quench their thirst.

u [have cared for me], and how you also especially have cared for me from youth on. Ah, my God, are your gifts here in this world so lovely—even though, for the sake of our sin, they stand everywhere mixed with the thorns and thistles of the curse <note>A reference to Genesis 3:17-18, where God curses the ground after the Fall of Man.</note>. Oh, how sweet your love and goodness should taste to us if we could enjoy them as a completely pure and heavenly food, separated from the feeling of earthly pleasure and the attractiveness of the creatures. Ah, this very thing—yes, this was one of those glorious beatitudes in which man found himself before the Fall in **paradisiacal innocence**. Your goodness and love had indeed then richly set before us all kinds of beautiful fruits from the external world to enjoy; but the life of our **free spirit** <note>original: "freyer Geist." In Pietist thought, this refers to the higher soul that is meant to remain unattached to material things.</note> did not consist in such **earthly foods** during those blessed times. The **spirit of nature** <note>original: "Natur-Geist." The lower part of the human constitution that governs physical life and bodily needs.</note> could well nourish itself from them, but without stain and without the descending craving and desire of our free spirit—which would not allow itself to be drawn down at all by the loveliness of earthly creatures nor be bound in love to the earthly. 

Oh, what a dominion over the creatures this was! Here, food was taken from external nature, but the free spirit of man stood completely indifferent toward external nature, and truly did not know what was evil or good, pleasant or foul-tasting within it. The creature could not take him captive with its loveliness, but simply had to serve as food for the outer person. But the true food of his spirit, which nourished him, which pastured him, and in which he delighted, was your unspeakable **love of God**, my God and Father, and a constant feeling of a heavenly pleasure in your divine light. Ah, in this **heavenly food** consists not only the true life of the free spirit, but also the outer person would have always partaken in it

## The 111th Psalm.

->*Thanksgiving for God’s bodily and spiritual blessing.*<-

**I** thank the Lord with my whole heart, in the council of the godly, and in the congregation.

Great are the works of the Lord; whoever heeds them has pure delight in them.

What he ordains is laudable and glorious, and his righteousness endures forever.

He has established a remembrance of his wonders, the gracious and merciful GOD.

He gives food to those who fear him; he remembers his covenant forever.

He lets his powerful deeds be proclaimed to his people, that he may give them the inheritance of the nations.

The works of his hands are truth and justice; all his commandments are upright.

They are maintained forever and ever, and are done faithfully and honestly.

He sends a redemption to his people; he promises that his covenant shall remain forever; holy and sublime <note>original: "heer" (modern: "hehr"). An archaic term meaning noble, majestic, or august.</note> is his name.

## A Prayer

*For the true spiritual and right use of earthly foods.*

->Psalm 111:4, 5<-

> *He has established a remembrance of His wonders, the gracious and merciful GOD; He gives food to those who fear Him.*

Dearest Lord JESUS, You are the self-existing Word <note>original: "selbständige Wort" — referring to Christ as the Logos, the autonomous and eternal Word of God.</note> of Your heavenly Father, and this divine Word is spirit and life. Therefore, You are also the bread that has come from heaven and gives life to the world. Ah, let me hunger only for this food, which shall be a true nourishment for me into all eternity. Alas! Until now, my spirit has sought to sustain its life only in external nature, and I have thus separated myself from the true life found in You and in Your Word. Grant, therefore, Lord JESUS, that I may offer back to You in true self-denial all earthly foods—which I have, as it were, stolen from You through my own presumption and preferred over the enjoyment of Your love—so that I may thus spiritually find again the Paradise from which Adam was physically cast out through the path of worldly pleasure and self-assertion. To that end, dearest Savior, let me henceforth enjoy all earthly foods of external nature in true detachment and denial of created things <note>original: "Verläugnung der Creaturen" — a spiritual practice of not letting one's heart become attached to worldly objects or people above God.</note>, and help me so that such enjoyment occurs without any further self-presumption, but rather that the free spirit

How unhappy is he who does not hunger for You! How happy, on the other hand, is he who sighs for You without ceasing! O eternal Truth, You who satisfy the spirit without being consumed, You who indeed do not transform Yourself into the one who wishes to be satisfied by You—as earthly bread does—but rather transform him into Yourself, as a heavenly Philosopher’s Stone and as a divine Sun-Bread; give Yourself to me, and grant that I may run toward Your bosom with impetuosity and without tiring. For without You and outside of You I am unhappy, because all goods that are not **God** and **Jesus** must be called nothing other than a pitiable poverty and misery. Finally, let me think of Your word that You said to **Peter**: *When you are converted one day*—namely, to the true living bread—*and are strengthened by it, then strengthen also your brothers.* <note>A paraphrase of Luke 22:32.</note> Grant that I keep these words in my memory at all times. And since You have provided for me so richly and gloriously in physical matters from outer nature, grant that I do not close my heart to my brothers in return, but rather may gladly strengthen them with physical goods. But grant also that I may be found a good steward of Your various spiritual gifts of grace, and also serve my brothers with the gift I have received, from the heart—

# The Third Hour of Rest in GOD,
->*at the approaching time of night and refreshment for the weary body.*<-

---

->Isaiah 38:1<-
> **Set your house in order, for you shall die and not remain alive.**

My **GOD**, the dark night breaks in once again, and I do not know whether the long night of death might cover me even today. O most gracious **GOD**, when I must one day step to these gates of death and look into the great, wide eternity, what a fearful terror might then seize me. For my future well-being or woe will then **depend** <note>original: "dependiren" — a Latinate loanword common in 18th-century German scholarly and devotional writing.</note> on my life lived well or poorly until now, or rather, on my dying well or poorly. Ah! What in the world is more terrifying to a wrongdoer than the announcement of death? But should death not be many thousands of times more terrifying to me if I had to die in the uncertainty of that state which is to follow death? When I view death as a necessary consequence of our sins, it is already terrifying enough. Were You not Yourself anxious, my Savior, until the baptism of death <note>This refers to the "baptism" of suffering mentioned by Jesus in Luke 12:50, often associated with his agony in Gethsemane.</note> was completed in You? Ah! whoever could have looked into Your heart back then, in this Your anxiety, he would—

<summary>This page concludes a prayer for spiritual service and begins a new evening meditation on mortality, using Isaiah's warning to King Hezekiah to reflect on the soul's preparedness for eternity.</summary>
<keywords>mortality, evening prayer, Isaiah 38, eternity, baptism of death, spiritual preparation</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the meditation from page 23 regarding Christ’s agony in Gethsemane. The previous page ended with a reflection on Jesus’s "baptism of death" and his suffering in the garden.</meta>

-hsemane, <note>The text begins mid-word, completing "Gethsemane" from the previous page.</note> which saw that which every one of us must, to some extent, feel at our own death. Yet, my **Jesus**, back then, when You were sorrowful even unto death, You took on my weakness so that You might clothe me with Your courage in my death. You descended into my helplessness so that I might rise up in Your strength. You burdened Yourself with my fear of death; but this fear was supported by Your divine power and is now for me a source of strength, courage, and confidence in my death. Your sorrow and fear were the scaffolding for the great altar of sacrifice, upon which You worked reconciliation <note>original: "Versœhnung" — the theological concept of making amends or bringing humanity back into harmony with God through Christ's sacrifice.</note> with Yourself for poor sinners standing in the fear of death; without this, it would be an audacity for me to hope for mercy. And since this reconciliation in Your blood does not depend <note>original: "dependiret" — a Latin loanword common in 18th-century German, emphasizing that the action relies entirely on an external source.</note> on me, but solely on Your merciful grace: oh, I will ceaselessly pray with a penitent heart for this grace, and thus daily prepare myself blessedly through true repentance for a hopeful death.

## The Second Devotion.

->*For the Gracious Impartation of Divine Life and Light.*<-

---

->Revelation 12:1.<- <note>original: "Offenb. 12, 1."</note>

->*A woman was clothed with the sun, and had the moon under her feet.*<-

You great and glorious God, you are the source of a living fire-power, you who from eternity have born your beloved Son as the Light of the World out of your divine essence. This great light likewise renews itself in its divine power throughout all eternity and never diminishes. And though you impart <term>impart: The original term "mittheilest" refers to the spiritual process of God sharing His own divine life and qualities with the human soul.</term> life to the whole world through this light, and nourish and water all things from your divine power: yet you remain an immutable light and an inexhaustible fire-source, from which one fullness of power and grace after another streams toward us, so that we may truly taste everywhere how kind you are. Here let us recognize why your Son commanded us to pray and say: *Our Father.* For you are thus the true Father of all good gifts in the realm of nature and grace. Ah, that I might now be deemed worthy to be your bride, who has trodden the dark moon of earthly worldly splendor under her feet and, clothed with the sun of your grace, may walk in your light. Ah, you light of my soul, let me remain under your rays and transfigure me into your image, so that, permeated by the fire of your love, I may cast off the brittle carnal mind <note>The "brittle carnal mind" refers to the human nature that is fragile, temporary, and resistant to spiritual transformation.</note> and, on the contrary, become a partaker of your divine nature and be found proven before you as pure, well-refined gold. Amen!

### The Third Devotion.
#### A Contemplation of the Infinite Glory of God.

---

->Revelation 1, 8.<- <note>original: "Offenb. 1, 8."</note>

*I am the Alpha and the Omega, the beginning and the end.* <note>original: "Ich bin das A und O, der Anfang und das Ende."</note>

O you eternal and unfathomable depth of divine majesty and glory, here I lay myself down in humility before you and wish to admire your infinite highness with the covered eyes of my poor human reason. Ah, I see daily before me the great <term>World-Edifice: The "Welt-Gebæude" refers to the entire created universe viewed as a grand, structured building designed by God.</term> world-edifice with its infinite riches, which must nevertheless come to an end. But you shall remain, you who are the beginning without beginning, and the end without end. Therefore, send me great strength from your infinite divine goodness to tear my heart away from all transitory things of an earthly world-joy, and to renounce the fleeting splendor of its hollow treasures. Do this so that, when the world passes away and all its riches fall away, I may encounter an imperishable good in the bosom of your infinite mercy, which may satisfy me eternally and make me perfectly rich. Amen!

*My God, if the heart wishes to delight itself too much*
*In gold and other treasures which this world gives us:*
*Then let me think that it all soon turns to dust,*
*And remains no longer than snow in the field.*

For through the Devil’s deception, we too have strayed and become lustful, seeking to recognize in the **Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil** the separation of the **fire’s wrath** and the **light**, or the difference between evil and good. We sought to gaze upon our created power and authority—which we should have used under the governance of the creatures in the light of divine love and simplicity—with self-complacency, to exalt ourselves with it, and thus to place ourselves in proud **self-love** beside you, you great GOD. Alas! By this, the fire- and light-properties of our souls, which stood in lovely harmony according to your image, were thrown into pure disorder. The **fire’s wrath** now gained the upper hand within us; fire and light were divided, good and evil were revealed, and the poisonous **serpent’s seed** was fully sown. Because we, in such properties that rise up against your gentle light, cannot possibly be your true children of light and of the sun—much less can we possibly partake in your divine **Tincture of light and love** <term>Tincture: In Christian alchemy and theosophy, this refers to a spiritual medicine or "essence" that transforms the soul, much like a medicinal tincture heals the body.</term> for the improvement of our hearts and the enrichment of our souls with all kinds of heavenly goods: Oh, let us first become truly humble and poor again, so that you may hereafter make us truly great and rich in yourself. Your **Tincture of love** will not enter where the swollen dross of self-love has, as it were, coated the metal of the heart and promotes the brittleness of the fleshly mind. Therefore, tread the serpent’s seed of this proud self-love ruling within us under your feet. And then, come to our aid with your heavenly **Tincture**, and soothe the **fire-wrath** stirred up by Satan, so that your divine image, in the fire- and light-properties harmonizing lovelily once more, may be introduced into us anew through your **regenerating Spirit**. Thus we shall also laud and praise you forever and ever. Amen!

<summary>This page describes the human fall as a descent into "self-love" and spiritual discord, where the soul's natural "fire" became a wrathful force. It concludes with a prayer for the "Tincture of Love" to restore divine harmony and humility.</summary>

<keywords>Fall of Man, self-love, Fire-wrath, Tincture of Love, Jacob Boehme, spiritual alchemy, regeneration, humility</keywords>

It is a crown that You can just as easily knock from my head if I become conceited as You were able to place it there. Let me recognize that You will leave those people empty of all goods of grace, fortune, and blessing who seek such goods through their own self-will without You, and who wish to claim them by their own strength. # The Ninth Devotion.

->For gracious protection from evil thoughts and distractions of the heart.<-

->Revelation 11:2.<-
->*The heathens <note>original: "Heyden." In this context, it refers to worldly or ungodly influences that invade the sanctity of the soul.</note> shall trample the holy city.*<-

**Dearest** Immanuel <note>Hebrew: "God with us."</note>, my heart should rightfully be a holy city, wherein You alone dwell and walk. And all my desires should be gathered here under the scepter of Your kingdom. Oh, how then would the thoughts of my heart stand in a lovely peace, stillness, and order. But alas! I have departed from the true harmony with Your divine stillness and rest, and have fallen into my *own* self—into my false **self-love** <term>Eigen-Liebe: A key concept in German mysticism referring to the ego's tendency to center itself rather than God; it is seen as the root of all spiritual distraction.</term> and my own will. Now my own thoughts are found to be those which trample the holy city of my heart, so that neither temple nor altar is cleansed, and the sacrifices of worship in the spirit are no longer performed therein; instead, the idols of world-love and self-love are set up there. Oh, let these images of Satan be destroyed within me through Your **Blood of the Lamb**, and gather all my scattered heart’s desires back to You. Then, when I one day carry the palms of victory in my hands as a testimony of defeated enemies, I will praise and glorify You in eternity. Amen!

# The Tenth Devotion.
## *Concerning a submissive surrender of our will into the will of God.*

---

->Revelation 14:4.<-
> *They follow the Lamb wherever he goes.*

Oh, dear Lord Jesus, when will the time come when no one else shall speak in my heart but You, and I will wish to hear no other voice but Yours? Oh, the constant noise of my earthly affairs <note>original: "Verrichtungen" — referring to the daily tasks, duties, or business of the world.</note> seeks to completely deafen me, and my desires cry out so loudly within me that I am unable to hear Your voice or perceive Your will when You call me to follow You wherever You go. Oh, what rebellion and resistance are found in my heart that speaks against it! But exactly from this, dear Savior, comes all the disorder and unrest of my heart. For all things that I seek outside of myself and outside of You cannot calm me, and the senses of the heart, once turned away from You, then only become more entangled and confused in the love of the world and of the flesh. Oh, therefore bend my will under Your gentle yoke, so that *Your will be done by me on earth as it is in heaven.* <note>A reference to the Lord's Prayer (Matthew 6:10).</note> Let me recognize the blessedness <term>Seligkeit: A state of spiritual joy, peace, and salvation; the ultimate happiness of the soul in God.</term> of those who do Your will. Thus shall it be a light burden to me, through which I shall find eternal rest for my soul. Amen!

# The Eleventh Devotion.

->*Concerning an earnest attentiveness to the Father's drawing toward true rest in Christ.*<-

---

->Revelation 3:20.<-

> Behold, I stand at the door and knock; if anyone hears my voice and opens the door, I will come in to him and eat with him, and he with me.

Most gracious God, despite all the pleasure I seek in the world, I am never at rest. For the severe fire of the divine will in my conscience causes my thoughts to accuse one another and leaves my heart in a languishing drought. Indeed, under this, Your divine drawing <note>original: "Zug" — a theological term referring to the internal "pull" or "attraction" exerted by God the Father to lead the soul toward Christ (based on John 6:44).</note>, my soul longs for its rest. But I have sought it in the world and its joy, where no true rest was to be found. While You, Lord Jesus, meanwhile knock at my door and wish to prepare a better meal for me. Therefore, if I continue to feel the drawing of Your heavenly Father, and a bitter fire of wrath from the Father’s character <note>original: "Eigenschaft" — in the mystical tradition of writers like Jakob Böhme, this refers to a specific quality or "mode" of God's being, here specifically the aspect of justice or judgment that humbles the soul.</note> sets my soul into a languishing sadness: then let me not be like the inexperienced Samuel, who did not know God’s voice and took his refuge in Eli <note>A reference to 1 Samuel 3, where the boy Samuel mistakes the voice of God for his mentor Eli.</note>. Let me rather go into the innermost part of my heart, pay heed to Your knocking grace, and say with this Samuel: "Speak, Lord, for Your servant wishes to hear no longer the voice of this world, but Your voice, and to enjoy in You the supper of a blessed heavenly rest." You, dearest Jesus, are indeed the self-existent Word, whom Your Father in heaven has commanded us to listen to. This, Your Word, is spirit and life, and when we [remain] in You, as the essential Word...

remain, then we remain at our food and nourishment, like a child at the breast of its mother. ## The Fourteenth Devotion.
### For True, Heartfelt Love Toward Our Neighbor.

->Revelation 5:9<-

> *The Lamb has redeemed us to God with his blood out of*

O you eternal Love and outflow of the heavenly Father, you have offered yourself for us all as a sacrifice of reconciliation <term>Versœhnung: the theological concept of Atonement, or the "making at one" of God and humanity</term>, so that the unsearchable divine love of the Father [might be poured] into our hearts through the Holy Spirit

...Spirit might be poured out. Then let me also rightly use this great salvation <term>Seligkeit: blessedness or the state of being saved; often implies a present joy in God’s presence</term>, which you give me through your indwelling <term>Einwohnung: the mystical concept of Christ or the Spirit living within the believer's heart</term>, for love toward you and all people. Pour out this love, as your noble God-life, fully into my heart through your Holy Spirit for the glorification <term>Verklärung: transfiguration or the process of being made radiant by God’s glory</term> of your great majesty in me.

->Amen!<-

# The Fifteenth Devotion.
## Concerning True Rest of the Soul in Union with GOD.

---

> Revelation 21:3, 4.
> 
> Behold, a tabernacle <note>original: "Hütte"; often used in German Bibles to denote the "dwelling" or "tent" of God among humanity</note> of God with men, and he will dwell with them, and they shall be his people, and God with them will be their God, and God will wipe away all tears from their eyes, and death shall be no more, nor sorrow, nor crying, nor pain shall be any more. For the former things have passed away.

**D**earest Lord JESUS, in you the old has passed away—the old heaven and earth of our corrupted heart which lay under the curse, along with the stormy sea of our confused passions and desires. But in you, all things shall also become new. The heart shall be a new heaven and a new earth, indeed, even a tabernacle in which you desire to dwell and take all sinful misery from us. Also, the sea of our restless desires shall be no more. Yes, you gladly wish to make us truly beautiful, to wipe away the tears, to drive out death, and to take away the pains, so that we may be your bride, adorned by you yourself, with whom you dwell in sweet *union of love* <term>Liebes-Vereinigung: a central concept in mystical theology describing the soul's highest state of intimacy and oneness with the Divine</term>. And when you embrace us with your sweet *kiss of love* <note>The "kiss of love" is a traditional metaphor for spiritual ecstasy and the communication of grace, derived from the Song of Solomon</note>: then you let the breath and spirit of your mouth—the breathing of your divine power and the divine ray of your glory, the radiance of your eternal image, which is your good Spirit—go forth like a poured-out ointment and balm of life, so that the bride, in the inner man, becomes ever more beautiful and, as if through a

an indwelling tincture <term>Tinctur: in the mystical tradition of Jakob Böhme and his followers, a spiritual essence or "dye" that penetrates and transforms the inner nature of a person</term> is ever increased in the powers of the spirit. Ah, my Savior, may I always keep my heart open here in true desire of faith, to receive this your **heavenly dew**, so that in me also everything that is old may pass away, yes, become entirely new. Without you, my whole heart lies in complete ruin. Therefore, help graciously through the all-soothing **love-balsam** of your blood, that I may no longer be led into such temptations of my flesh, but that I may go out from the fire of my self-entangling and consuming nature, penetrate into your peace, come from one clarity to another, and be satisfied in the contemplation of your countenance, when your perfect image is awakened and enlightened in me according to the mature age of the spirit <note>original: "männlichen Alter des Geistes"; a reference to Ephesians 4:13, suggesting a state of spiritual maturity or the "full stature" of Christ</note>. Thus it will go eternally well for my soul. Amen!

<meta>This page concludes the Sixteenth Devotion, continuing the metallurgical metaphor of the soul as dross-filled metal needing refinement. [before the] Fall we were in your hand like a sparkling gemstone and like pure gold. For we stood in the fellowship of your light. But now, since we have turned away from this light of yours, our heart is darkened, and our soul is corrupted with the impure dross of sinful desires. And I find in my soul an image of an impure and completely dross-filled metal, which consumes itself in the fire of its evil desires. The scattered desires of my heart are so daily entangled with one another that I am far removed from all true soul-rest. Now Lord JESUS, you have commanded that I should buy gold from you for this my misery. So let your holy blood be such a living, such a liquid gold for me, which may completely penetrate me, cleanse me from all the torments of sin, elevate the powers of the spirit in me, and thus transform me into a pure, refined gold that stands firm in all trials of your divine fire of love. Ah yes, Lord JESUS, you are made for me also for righteousness and for redemption. *Deliver me then from all evil, and help me through to your heavenly kingdom. To you be glory from eternity to eternity. Amen!*

## The Seventeenth Devotion.
### *That God, after complete destruction, wants to*
### *fully glorify and ennoble us.*

->Revelation 21:5.<-

> *Behold, I make all things new, and these words are*
> *faithful and true.*

**G**reat GOD and Lord of heaven and earth, your wisdom has so ordered it in your entire kingdom, that everything first goes into death and <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: "putrefaction"; in the context of both alchemy and mystical theology, this refers to the necessary decay or "breaking down" of the old form before a new, perfected life can emerge</note>

<meta>This page continues the Seventeenth Devotion. The previous page established that all things must undergo death and decay ("putrefaction") to be renewed; this page concludes the prayer with a focus on the necessity of breaking down the physical body to reach "paradisiacal clarity."</meta>

must, from which something nobler and better shall afterwards arise. Your Son **JESUS** Christ himself was the grain of wheat, which could not give us wholesome fruit until it had died. <note>A reference to John 12:24: "Unless a grain of wheat falls into the earth and dies, it remains alone; but if it dies, it bears much fruit."</note> Yes, your divine counsel has seen fit to also set our spirit free from the bonds of the flesh through the <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: "putrefaction"; in alchemy, this refers to the breakdown or "blackening" of matter, which is the necessary first step before it can be purified into gold.</note> of self-denial and through the mortification of the "old Adam" <note>A theological term for the sinful human nature inherited from the Fall.</note> and to prepare it for a better glory. Oh, then let everything that cannot inherit your kingdom in us be crucified with Christ. And I thus desire no rest or comfort for my flesh and blood, which I must regard as bonds that still hold the poor spirit captive under the service of vanity. 

Because, however, the curse cannot be completely removed from external nature—and thus also from the miserable hut of my external person—during this earthly life, our bodies (being mixed with curse and impurity) must first be <term>resolved</term> <note>original: "resolviret"; to be broken down into constituent parts or dissolved.</note> through death and <term>putrefaction</term> back into their original <term>principles</term> <note>original: "principia"; the fundamental elements or building blocks of matter.</note> of the earth from which they arose. There they must be changed and <term>transplanted</term> until finally, on 그날 [that day] when all elements shall melt in fire and find their first purity again, you will also bring our bodies back to paradisiacal clarity through this final transformation in a violent <term>resolution</term> <note>original: "Resolution"; here meaning the chemical-like dissolution or breaking apart of the old form to make way for the new.</note> of fire. 

Ah, then give grace that I may heartily long for such total restoration, and let me recognize your goodness and great wisdom even here in these your judgments, which you will still let pass over the body of my external person through death and <term>putrefaction</term>. May I, in such deep and hidden ways of yours, never doubt this truth: that nevertheless from you, through you, and to you are all things. Therefore let your name be lauded and praised
->in eternity. Amen!<-

# The Eighteenth Devotion.

*For complete liberation from the service of vanity through the redemption of our bodies.*

---

->Revelation 21:1.<-

->And I saw a new heaven and a new earth.<-

You great Redeemer and Restorer of all things, since your poor creature is still held back in the bonds of its body from attaining the perfect and glorious freedom of the children of God, inasmuch as it is subjected to the service of vanity <note>original: "Dienst der Eitelkeit"; referring to Romans 8:20. In this context, "vanity" signifies the transience, futility, and corruption of the physical world after the Fall.</note>, even against its will, through the incessant cravings of our flesh and blood, until finally this mortal thing shall be swallowed up by the immortal. Oh, grant then that we may long with true anxiety <note>The author uses "ängstlich," which in older German implies an earnest, pained, and deep spiritual yearning.</note> for the redemption of our body. Do not let our spirit be constantly held back by a wretched love for our bodies, but let us view them as earthly vessels and fragile huts <note>A common 17th-century metaphor for the human body, emphasizing its temporary and precarious nature.</note> that cannot endure forever. Let us recognize our gain in temporal death, and desire from our hearts to live outside the flesh—indeed, to be at home with you. Raise our weary strengths to tear themselves more and more away from the tenderness <note>original: "Zärtlichkeit"; here meaning an over-fondness for physical comfort or a self-indulgent attachment to the body's needs.</note> of the flesh: Because we must be perfected only through the lowliness of the outward man and the weakness of earthly life. Finally, let the longed-for time come when the revelation of your glorious kingdom in the new earth will also produce a new earth within us, and clothe us with a spiritual body, after the old body has found its decay in death. ->Amen!<-

<vocab>Devotion, Liberation, Vanity, Redemption, Revelation, Amen</vocab>

<summary>This page begins the Eighteenth Devotion, presenting a prayer for the soul to be freed from its attachment to the physical body. It reflects the Pietist and mystical desire to view the body as a temporary "fragile hut" that must pass away to allow for a spiritual rebirth.</summary>
<keywords>redemption of the body, vanity, spiritual body, new earth, liberation, Pietism, transience</keywords>

# The Nineteenth Devotion.

->*For the blessed achievement of victory over the external man, who holds the spirit captive through the outward senses.*<-

---

->Revelation 21:7.<-

->*He who overcomes shall inherit all things.*<-

Dearest LORD JESUS, all the sensory pleasures of the external nature, which are stirred up through the senses of my body, are prone to kill the soul and take the spirit captive. Smell, taste, sight, and hearing are windows through which death climbs in <note>This is a reference to Jeremiah 9:21: "For death is come up into our windows, and is entered into our palaces." In 17th-century spiritual literature, the five senses were often portrayed as vulnerable entry points for sin.</note>. Oh, from this violent slavery of the spirit within the workings of external nature, may you deliver me by your grace; and grant that I may gladly renounce all the treasures, riches, pleasures, and glories of the world, and that the longing of my spirit may instead be directed only toward heavenly treasures. Let me, therefore, wrestle valiantly for the prize of heavenly freedom, so that your victorious and triumphant kingdom may also be revealed in my soul, and that the dominion of the <term>inner man</term> <note>original: "innern Menschens"; a concept from 2 Corinthians 4:16. It refers to the spiritual self that is being renewed, in contrast to the "outer man" or physical self that is perishing.</note>, which is the life from GOD, may also break through to the external man, to remain likewise subject to you and to die gradually upon the cross. Finally, when I shall have fought the fight of faith here and overcome: then let me also sit with you upon the throne of your glory, and inherit the kingdom that is promised and prepared for us through your great love. Amen!

> Ah! what is earthly gold, which this world so adores,
> My spirit seeks that treasure which God in heaven stores.
> Rise, rise! swing yourself, my spirit, toward that eternity,
> For what is temporal here vanishes with time’s brevity.

# The Twentieth Devotion.
## For the blessed entry into the Kingdom of Glory.

---

### Revelation 22:20.

> He who testifies to these things says, "Surely I am coming soon."
> Amen. Even so, come, Lord Jesus.

Dearest Lord JESUS, you have indeed revealed yourself as our <term>Mediator</term> <note>A theological term for Jesus’s role as the bridge between God and humanity.</note> in the flesh; but were justified only in the Spirit <note>A reference to 1 Timothy 3:16, "God was manifest in the flesh, justified in the Spirit."</note>. The new creature of the **inner man** is indeed already established through your grace in your believers; but it has not yet pleased you to bring the <term>restoration</term> <note>original: "Wiederbringung"; in this context, it refers to the spiritual and physical restitution of the world to its state before the Fall.</note> of the **outer man** and of external nature in this world to its completion. Our body is still dead and lies under the curse for the sake of sin. But your believers nevertheless have the hope that you will also one day make their mortal bodies alive, because your Spirit already dwells in them. Through your great omnipotence, you will surely accomplish this all at once on that great <term>Day of Separation</term> <note>original: "Scheidungs-Tage"; refers to the Last Judgment, when the saved are separated from the lost and the soul is finally separated from the corruption of the physical world.</note> and free my body from the disorder of the **Fall**. Oh, Lord JESUS, give to me also a heartfelt longing for the revelation of the children of God in this glorious freedom. Let me lift up my head daily and look for the redemption of my body. Yes, come, Lord JESUS, if it pleases you, and direct my course so that I may come to meet you.
->Amen!<-

# Heart Poured Out Before GOD at the Daily Evening Sacrifice,

## *Consisting of various examinations conducted according to the holy Lord’s Prayer, namely, whether the past day was spent for the glory of God.*

---
### The First Examination.
### *Our Father.*
---

Prepare yourself, my heart, in this evening hour to still present the account of how you have walked before your GOD throughout this day. The prayer of your dear Son <note>A reference to the Lord's Prayer (the "Our Father"), which Jesus taught his disciples.</note> shall be my judge today and convince me before your divine throne that I have often requested something with my mouth according to its content, for which, however, the heart did not long. Ah <term>Our Father</term> <note>original: "Vater unſer"</note>, dear Father, you Father of my Lord JESUS Christ, how ashamed I stand before you when I ask myself whether I am even now your true child, since I have called you Father? Children are of a similar mind to their father. But you, dear Father, are pure love and mercy through and through, even toward your enemies. And I, on the other hand, find in my whole life still so many traces of a loveless heart. Ah Father, I am not worthy to be called your child! Yet you are still the same Father who took back that <term>prodigal son</term> <note>A reference to the parable in Luke 15, where a father welcomes home a wayward child with open arms.</note>. Therefore, for the sake of your Son JESUS Christ, open your fatherly bosom and take me again into your arms of grace, and so I will find rest for my soul.

->Amen!<-

**Vocabulary:** <term>Evening Sacrifice</term> <note>original: "Abend-Opfer"; a metaphor common in Pietist devotion, likening private evening prayer to the ritual sacrifices in the ancient Temple.</note>, <term>Lord’s Prayer</term> <note>original: "Vater Unſer"</note>, <term>Examination</term> <note>original: "Prüfung"; a spiritual "audit" or checking of one's conscience.</note>, Heart, GOD, JESUS Christ, Mercy, Prayer, Arms of Grace.

# The Heart Poured Out Before GOD at the Daily Evening Sacrifice,
### *Consisting of various examinations conducted according to the holy Our Father, namely, whether the past day was spent to the glory of God.*

---

## The First Examination.
### *Our Father.*

Prepare yourself, my heart, in this evening hour to still present the account of how you have walked before your GOD throughout this day. The prayer of your dear Son shall today be my judge and convince me before your divine throne that, according to its content, I have often requested something with my mouth for which the heart nevertheless did not long. Ah Our Father, dear Father, you Father of my Lord JESUS Christ, how ashamed I stand before you when I ask myself whether I am even now your true child, because I called you Father? Children are like-minded with their father. You, however, dear Father, are through and through pure love and mercy even toward your enemies. And I, on the contrary, still find in my whole life so many traces of a loveless heart. Ah Father, I am not worthy that I should be called your child! <note>A reference to the confession of the Prodigal Son in Luke 15:19.</note> Yet you are still the same Father who took back that <term>prodigal son</term> <note>original: "verlohrnen Sohn."</note>. Therefore, for the sake of your Son JESUS Christ, open your fatherly <term>bosom</term> <note>original: "Schoos," referring to a place of protection, comfort, and intimacy, often used in a parental or divine sense.</note> and take me again into your arms of grace; so shall I find rest for my soul.

->Amen!<-

## The Second Examination.

->*Our.*<-

Now I truly become aware of the lovelessness of my heart, since I have so often dared to even presume to claim you for myself alone, and to call you only *my* Father, even though you are not only mine but the Father of us all. If it had been possible, I would have gladly long ago appropriated the rights of a child in your house for myself alone, and, conversely, in my lack of love, regarded my brothers as servants. I would have gladly believed that I had the right to claim a greater share of your worldly goods, had you not just now, with your *Our Father*, put a stop to my far-reaching and self-appropriating desires. For here, loving Fatherly-heart, you have commanded me to include my brothers, and practically prescribed that I wish and grant them all good things in my prayer. 

But I cannot hide it from your all-seeing eyes: all my thoughts, all my worries, yes, all my striving and yearning <note>original: "Dichten und Trachten," a traditional biblical idiom referring to the innermost inclinations of the heart; see Genesis 8:21.</note> has even this very day been directed toward myself alone. I have not thought of the needs <term>Nothdurft</term> <note>A term referring to the basic physical necessities of life—food, clothing, and shelter—required to relieve distress.</note> of my poor brothers. Ah, I notice well that it often feels burdensome just to listen to the sighs of the poor. Oh, how much more difficult <note>original: "vvie ſauer," literally "how sour," an idiom meaning arduous, bitter, or requiring great effort.</note> it would be for me to take their sighs into my very heart, to pour out their distress as my own distress before you, and thus to pray the "Our Father" with its true emphasis. My GOD, restrain the self-love dwelling within me, which is a poisonous source of all my sinful 
->actions.<- <note>The original German word "Hand-lungen" is hyphenated as it breaks across to the next page.</note>

# The Third Examination.

### *Who art in heaven.*

May I indeed dare to lift my eyes toward you, dear Father? You are in heaven, and I, conversely, am on earth; I cling with my heart to the earth and am earthly-minded. Ah, what fellowship then has heaven with the earth? If you are my Father, who dwells in heaven and has set aside a heavenly inheritance for his children, then should I not also have looked upon heaven today with joy and delight—yes, with the most inward love? Why then have I kicked this happiness away from me with my feet and remained stuck until now to the stinking earth? Why have I wanted to set my heart firmly only upon mountains of sand and snow <note>The author uses "mountains of sand and snow" as a metaphor for worldly ambitions and possessions—things that appear grand but are unstable, cold, and easily swept away or melted.</note> and chosen this transitory existence for my treasure and portion? Ah, even this day has not passed without such carnal desires, and my mind is often like a tempestuous sea that cannot be still <note>original: "ungestümes Meer," likely an allusion to Isaiah 57:20, which compares the wicked to a tossing sea that cannot rest.</note>. Ah, heavenly Father, tear me loose from the earth and lay me in your lap, so that I may find rest for my soul.

---

### The Sixth Examination.

## *Thy will be done, on earth as it is in heaven.*

It is not unknown to me, my GOD, that your will is done at all times. I know very well the perfect power that you have over all your creatures, and you do with me, in me, and through me what you will, even without my own willing. But what does it profit me if I do your will as a slave, and not as a child, in true love, with a willing spirit, and without false intentions. Where is my zeal to recognize and fulfill your will in such a way? ## The Seventh Examination.
### *Give us this day our daily bread.*

O you Father of all good and perfect gifts, if I had recognized the necessity of seeking my bread from you alone, I would have more often called upon you for it with greater humility. But I have many times asked you for bread and yet remained unconcerned whether you wished to give it to me or deny it. For I have always secretly flattered myself that I could well enough provide for myself. Whom have I therefore served more: you or Mammon? <note>Mammon is a biblical term, often personified as a false god, representing wealth, avarice, and material possessions.</note> It is this latter that has caused me to send you nothing but lukewarm desires and cold-hearted prayers, while on the other hand I sacrificed all my effort, strength, and skill to the former. ...to win. It is true, my GOD, you have in part favored us poor humans in the world and set us over others, so that good order and governance <note>original: *Regiment*; this refers to the exercise of authority and the maintenance of social or political rule.</note> might be maintained and evil punished. But you also demand a spirit of love and gentleness. Even here, no ungodly zeal or impure bitterness should interfere, which violates the foundation of love that we owe even to wrongdoers. My GOD, test me here yourself, whether I have perhaps encroached upon love and gentleness in my station <note>original: *Stande*; referring to one's social rank, office, or professional position and the behaviors expected of it.</note> and lead me back to the right path. Let your love be poured out over me in the gentle heavenly dew of your good Spirit, so that I may share a heart full of love with my poor brothers in return.

## The Ninth Examination.

### *And lead us not into temptation.*

Dearest GOD, here I have often been my own enemy. The senses of my body are like domestic thieves and traitors who open the door of the heart to Satan and the world, and abandon my soul as prey to evil desires. Examine, merciful Father, my otherwise necessary enjoyment of food and drink, whether it has not often extended too far and become an arbitrary indulgence. And perhaps you also find something frivolous and meddlesome <note>original: *fürvvitziges*; implies an impertinent or transgressive curiosity that oversteps boundaries.</note> in those desires that hide under the name of science and knowledge <note>original: *Wissenschafft und Erkæntnis*; in this era, "science" referred to all systematic learning and academic study, not just the natural sciences.</note>. How easily an excessive zeal to explore divine mysteries, to search through the secrets of nature, and to pry into other curiosities <note>original: *Curiositæten*; at the time, this term often referred to rare objects or obscure topics that were fascinating but potentially distracting from spiritual life.</note>...

...to look at or listen to, crossing the permitted boundaries. At the very least, it is certain that all learning <note>original: *Gelehrsamkeit*</note> without the anointing <note>original: *Salbung*; refers to the spiritual empowerment or grace given by the Holy Spirit</note> of the Holy Spirit is truly to be called a "Tree of Knowledge," which is lovely and beautiful from the outside and appears as if it would make one wise, but which afterwards brings death to many and plunges them into great blindness. If I then, dear Father, have become weak in such temptations of my flesh: what would have happened if high trials <note>original: *Anfechtungen*; a heavy term in German theology referring to spiritual crises, attacks by the devil, or deep doubts</note> and the fiery arrows of the Evil One should fall upon me? Oh, then the fire of trial would surely discover nothing but lead and copper in me, and conversely, little gold of a fire-resistant faith would be found in me to remain in the crucible of temptation. O LORD, grant me strength to watch and pray, so that I do not fall into temptation.

## The Tenth Examination.

### *But deliver us from evil.*

My GOD, have I truly thought of my death today and yearned to be delivered once and for all from this wicked world? I must indeed confess that the land in which we dwell in this life is a hostile land; Satan, our irreconcilable enemy, is prince in this land, and even all our passions are his allies who fight against us. Oh wretched man, why then do you not yearn for the heavenly fatherland, and why do you still love the "Egypt" of this world so dearly? It is certainly a sorrowful sign that for the sake of its flesh-pots <note>original: *Fleisch-Töpfe*; a biblical allusion to the Israelites longing for the comforts of their former life in Egypt despite their enslavement (Exodus 16:3)</note> I [still]... Egypt’s slavery and wretched service for straw <note>original: *Stoppel-Dienst*; a reference to the Israelites being forced to gather stubble or straw to make bricks under Pharaoh (Exodus 5:12)</note>...

## The Eleventh Examination.
### *For Thine is the kingdom.*

## The Twelfth Examination.
### *Thine is the power and the glory forever.*

Great and mighty GOD, there is no power in us; we do not know what we should do, but our eyes must look to You in all things. And if I have therefore accomplished anything good even in my princely status <note>original: *Fürſtlichen Stande*; this indicates the author or the persona of the prayer is of high noble rank, likely a ruler or member of a royal court.</note> and government: then surely my heart should have agreed with my mouth and said: *Thine is the power, Thine is the glory.* If there is anything good in my life, it is truly Yours alone. But have I not perhaps also often sought my own honor and my own praise here? Oh, that I might truly feel a displeasure in my heart when others perhaps admire <note>original: *admiriren*; borrowed from the French/Latin, meaning to gaze at with wonder or flattery.</note> my arrangements and undertakings all too much. Oh, that I might notice the deception of the flesh here and over my own spirit

## The Thirteenth Examination.
### *Amen.*

Here, dear heart, examine your faith, and how you have spoken your *Amen* to your GOD until now. Oh, how difficult this *Amen* truly is. *Amen* is a word of the heart, which is spoken through pure sighs and longings of the spirit. *Amen* seals, through its sighs, the longing of the heart for that which we have prayed in the *Our Father* <note>The Lord's Prayer.</note>. And this holy longing storms heaven, penetrates through the veil to the Mercy Seat <term>Mercy Seat: In the Bible (Exodus 25), this was the gold lid of the Ark of the Covenant, representing the place where God meets humanity and grants forgiveness.</term>, reaches into the heart of GOD and pulls His help to itself. Oh, my soul, where has such a longing been within you until now? Where are your sighs? Where have the burning desires remained for that which you prayed for? Is not everything rather completely

...cover me with your wings, and my confidence will be in you; and truth is my weapon and shield against all enemies. Under your protection, I shall not need to fear the terror of the night, when it looks dark in my heart and the dark shadows of spiritual trials <note>original: "Anfechtungen"—a term used by Martin Luther and later Pietists to describe intense periods of spiritual struggle, doubt, or feeling abandoned by God.</note> spread themselves there. In such darkness, the enemy likes to approach the heart and wishes to make your union <note>A reference to the "unio mystica" or mystical union between the soul and God.</note> with me impossible.

But your shield teaches me that such darkness is like light before you—indeed, it is a preparation for the light. For in this dark wilderness, in this dark valley, I will not see and yet I will believe. I may well be deprived of your perceptible comfort <note>original: "empfindlichen Trostes"—this refers to spiritual joy that can be "felt" or experienced emotionally. Devotional writers often warned that God removes this feeling to test the believer's faith.</note>, but I will still be assured that you are with me and that your rod and staff comfort me. Yes, you **Lord JESUS**, my protection, will become ever brighter and more recognizable to me in dark faith—in faith without seeing.

So I also need not be afraid of the arrows that fly by day. For when I hide myself in my fortress and see how His truth covers me, I am safe from these arrows of Belial <note>A biblical term for the devil or personified wickedness.</note>. They fly by day when the sun of divine love shines most beautifully in my soul. Ah, when the inexperienced soul leaves its fortress because it thinks, in its presumed joy of faith, that there is no danger and that no fall could topple it, however great it may be: then these arrows are most harmful.

But through your grace, my **God**, I have learned to recognize them, and against them I am covered by you with truth and purity.

If I remain constantly in the Lord, I am certain that the creeping pestilence with its plague of lust <note>original: "Lust-Seuche"—while this term was often a medical name for syphilis in the 18th century, the author uses it here as a metaphor for the "contagion" of sinful desires or worldly passions that "infect" the soul.</note> will have to depart from me and may not touch me. The LORD has promised me that I shall see my desire, not only how the inward-stirring wickedness of my heart shall be driven out there before the eyes of the Almighty, but also how he will repay the godless from without, who creep about only in Satan's power to ruin souls.

Because the Lord has taken me into His gracious protection, He has now also commanded His strong angels to guard me on my ways. My ways, however, proceed in the Lord's truth and righteousness. Therefore, no evil shall touch me now.
